1. Starting from node A, observe the connections:
   - A connects to E, S, B, and F.

2. From B:
   - B connects to S, C, and G.

3. From E:
   - E does not have a clear path moving towards J immediately. It connects to B, F, and T, which are re-explored from another part of the graph.

4. From F:
   - F connects to A, B, and T.

5. Working towards node J from various connections:
   - J connects to G, I, and Q.
   - G connects to B, C, and H.
   - I connects to H and P.
   - Q connects to O, P, and R.

From node A, passing through B and G to reach J, or:
   - A connects to B (1 edge).
   - B connects to G (1 edge).
   - G connects to J (1 edge).

Combining these steps, the shortest path from A to J involves passing through B and G.

Thus, the shortest path length between the two black nodes A and J is:

Length: 3, Path: [A, B, G, J]

**Paths between A and I:**

1. A → E → B → T → H → I
2. A → E → B → T → F → Q → I
3. A → E → B → G → C → N
4. A → S → B → T → H → I
5. A → S → B → T → F → Q → I
6. A → S → B → G → C → R → J → Q → I
7. A → S → B → G → C → N

**Critical Nodes:**

By observing the paths:
1. Node B is a common node in multiple paths.
2. Node T connects both H and F, which are part of multiple paths to I.
3. Paths involving S → B → G → C → N still need to be truncated.

Since node B is a central hub, removing it will break many of these paths. Node T further strengthens the disconnection, and node N is also pivotal to ensure no alternate path exists.

By removing nodes B and T, paths involving nodes A and I are effectively disconnected.

Thus, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 2.

Answer: Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, T]

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the nodes \( A \) and \( T \) in the given graph, we need to analyze the paths connecting these two nodes.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:
1. **Identify Direct and Indirect Connections Between \( A \) and \( T \):**
    - From node \( A \), it directly connects to nodes: \( S, E, \text{and } F \).
    - From node \( T \), it directly connects to nodes: \( B \) and \( F \).
    - Since both \( F \) and \( B \) connect both \( A \) and \( T \) through paths, removing any of these connections eliminates one path between \( A \) and \( T \).

2. **Find all Paths Between \( A \) and \( T \):**
    - Path 1: \( A \rightarrow F \rightarrow T \)
    - Path 2: \( A \rightarrow E \rightarrow B \rightarrow T \)
    - Path 3: \( A \rightarrow F \rightarrow J \rightarrow Q \rightarrow I \rightarrow G \rightarrow B \rightarrow T \)

3. **Determine Minimum Edges to Remove for Complete Disconnection:**
    - Remove \( (F, T) \): This removes Path 1.
    - Remove \( (B, T) \): Subsequent points removing \( (E, B) \) or \( (F, B) \) which already take into account since only two are necessary.

By removing edges \( (F, B) \) and \( (F, T) \), all the paths linking \( A \) and \( T \) are broken. Therefore, we need only two strategic edges to completely disconnect \( A \) and \( T \).

### Conclusion:
The minimum number of edges that must be removed is:

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(F, B), (F, T)]
--- Ground Truth ---

- However, it does not eliminate all paths since there are some that still connect via other nodes.
